\phi_{y}
and the integration over
J_{y}
is simple, leaving us with

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)\epsilon_{x}^{2}}\int dJ_{x}d\phi_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{x}\cos\phi_{x}[1-q_{x}\sin 2(\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x})]\exp[-\frac{J_{x}[1-q_{x}\sin 2(\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x})]}{\epsilon_{x}}](1-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\sin 2(\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}))

\displaystyle\times\sin\left(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\left[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}(1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})\right]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\right)

This resembles the simplified version of Eq.(2.13) in the PR-AB 2018 paper.

Returning to the 2D form of the centroid in Eq.(
15.2.2
), the integrations over the phases
\phi_{x},\phi_{y}
have to be
done first. We write the last sine function in the integrand

\displaystyle\sin\left(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\left[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}(1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})+w_{xy}J_{y}(1+q_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}})\right]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\left(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+[q_{x}w_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-q_{y}w_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\tau-[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\sin(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

\displaystyle c_{xx}(J_{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{x}w_{xx}J_{x}\tau,\;\;\;c_{xy}(J_{y})=q_{y}w_{xy}J_{y}\tau,\;\;\;c_{00}=[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}]\tau+\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

Keeping terms to
O(q_{x})
, we can write

[1-q_{x}\sin 2(\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x})](1-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\sin 2(\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}))\simeq 1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}

Combining the phase dependent trigonometric terms

\displaystyle\cos\phi_{x}\sin(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

\displaystyle\times(1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\sin[2\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}]

\displaystyle+\sin[-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}])

\displaystyle\times(1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\sin\phi_{1}+\sin\phi_{2})-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\left[\cos(\phi_{1}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})-\cos(\phi_{1}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\cos(\phi_{2}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})-\cos(\phi_{2}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})\right]

where

\displaystyle\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{3}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{1}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{4}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{1}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=4\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{5}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{2}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=-2\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{6}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{2}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=2\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

The coefficients of
\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}
and
\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}
are the same for all the phases
\phi_{j}
.

The dipole moment in 2D is now

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

(15.30)

\displaystyle\times d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{1}{2}(\sin\phi_{1}+\sin\phi_{2})-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\left[\cos\phi_{3}-\cos\phi_{4}+\cos\phi_{5}-\cos\phi_{6}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\left\{\Phi_{1}+\Phi_{2}-\Phi_{3}+\Phi_{4}-\Phi_{5}+\Phi_{6}\right\}

(15.31)

where

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{1}

(15.32)

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{2}

(15.33)

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{3}

(15.34)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{4}

(15.35)

\displaystyle\Phi_{5}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{5}

(15.36)

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{6}

(15.37)

Thus the integrands in the terms
\Phi_{j}
, j=1,… 6 are respectively,

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i\{\phi_{1}-i\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-i\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp\left[i\{2\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i\{\phi_{2}-i\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-i\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp\left[i\{-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{4})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{4\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{5})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{-2\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{6})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{6}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{2\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

We have defined the constant complex parameters (independent of phase space variables)

z_{xx}=c_{xx}/J_{x}-i\frac{q_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}=\frac{q_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}(w_{xx}\tau\epsilon_{x}-i),\;\;\;z_{xy}=c_{xy}/J_{y}-i\frac{q_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}=\frac{q_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}(w_{xy}\tau\epsilon_{y}-i)

(15.38)

The parameters
z_{xx},z_{xy}
are constant, independent of the actions
J_{x},J_{y}
. We also have
w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}\tau\gg 1

and we must also require that
w_{xy}\tau\epsilon_{y}\gg 1
(the tune spread in
x
is determined both by
w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}

and
w_{xy}\epsilon_{y}
. Under these conditions,
|z_{xx}|\simeq q_{x}w_{xx}\tau
and
|z_{xy}|\simeq q_{y}w_{xy}\tau
.

The expansion into Bessel functions is the same in all the phases
\phi_{j}
, the differences are in the exponential factors in
the front of the form
\exp[i\{a_{j}\phi_{x}+b_{j}\Delta\phi_{x}\}]
,
j=1,...6
.

The integrations over
\phi_{x},\phi_{y}
are done using the result

\int d\phi\exp[im\phi]\exp[ia\sin(2\phi-\theta)]=2\pi J_{-m/2}(a)\exp[i(m/2)\theta]

which follow from the expansion

\exp[ia\sin\theta]=\sum_{k}J_{k}(a)\exp[ik\theta]

Hence

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Im}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\left(\exp\left[i\{2\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)\right)

The
\phi_{x}
and
\phi_{y}
integrations can be done separately

\displaystyle\int d\phi_{x}\exp\left[i\{2\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi\exp[-i(\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{00})]J_{-1}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[i2\Delta\phi_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\pi\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})

\displaystyle\int d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]=2\pi J_{0}(-z_{xy})=2\pi J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})

where we used
J_{0}(-z)=J_{0}(z),J_{-1}(z)=-J_{1}(z)
. Hence

\Phi_{1}(J_{x},J_{y})=-2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left\{\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right\}

(15.39)

We have

\displaystyle\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\omega_{x,0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}](t-\tau)-[\omega_{x,0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\omega_{x,0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}](t-2\tau)-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

The dominant term in this phase
\omega_{x,0}(t-2\tau)\rightarrow 0
at the echo time
t=2\tau
. Hence this term
\Phi_{1}

will have a significant contribution to the echo.

Continuing

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Im}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left(\exp\left[i\{-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]J_{0}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

The phase factor shows that it is a sub-dominant term and
\Phi_{2}
can be dropped from the echo amplitude.

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[i\{\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left\{\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{0}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right\}

This will contribute to the echo.

\displaystyle\Phi_{4}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{4\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-3\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{-2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[i4\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{-2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

This will contribute

\displaystyle\Phi_{5}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{-2\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[-i2\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

This will be sub-dominant and can be dropped.

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{2\phi_{x}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-3\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{-1}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[i2\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

This will be sub-dominant and can be dropped. Combining the 3 dominant terms

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\left\{\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{3}+\Phi_{4}\right\}

(15.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}{\rm Im}\left\{\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}{\rm Re}\left\{\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})][J_{0}(z_{xx}J_{x})-J_{2}(z_{xx}J_{x})]J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right\}

The 1D theory had shown that the 2nd and 3rd terms, i.e the contributions from
\Phi_{3},\Phi_{4}
are about 10% of that
from the 1st term, so they will be dropped.
Hence

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}{\rm Im}\left\{\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right\}

(15.42)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}{\rm Im}\left\{e^{i[\omega_{x,0}](t-2\tau)-q_{x}/2}\left[\int dJ_{x}\;J_{x}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}+iw_{xx}J_{x}(t-2\tau)]J_{1}(z_{xx}J_{x})\right]\right.

\displaystyle\times\left.\left[dJ_{y}\exp[-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}+iw_{xy}J_{y}(t-2\tau)]J_{0}(z_{xy}J_{y})\right]\right\}

Define the dimensionless variables

\displaystyle u_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{x}/\epsilon_{x},\;\;\;u_{y}=J_{y}/\epsilon_{y},\;\;\;\xi_{x}(t)=w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}(t-2\tau),\;\;\;\xi_{y}(t)=w_{xy}\epsilon_{y}(t-2\tau),

\displaystyle a_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-i\xi_{x},\;\;\;a_{y}=1-i\xi_{y},\;\;\;Q_{x}=z_{xx}\epsilon_{x},\;\;\;Q_{y}=z_{xy}\epsilon_{y},

\displaystyle\Phi_{x}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{x,0}(t-2\tau)

We have

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{K}\theta_{x}{\rm Im}\left\{e^{i(\Phi_{x}-q_{x}/2)}\left[\int du_{x}\;u_{x}\exp[-a_{x}u_{x}]J_{1}(Q_{x}u_{x})\right]\left[\int du_{y}\;\exp[-a_{y}u_{y}]J_{0}(Q_{y}u_{y})\right]\right\}

(15.43)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{K}\theta_{x}{\rm Im}\left\{e^{i(\Phi_{x}-q_{x}/2)}H_{1,1}(a_{x},Q_{x})H_{0,0}(a_{y},Q_{y})\right\}

where. as in the PR-AB paper, the functions
H_{m,n}(a,Q)
are defined as

H_{m,n}(a,Q)=\int_{0}^{\infty}du\;u^{m}\exp[-au]J_{n}(Qu)

The expression for the centroid is the same as in Eq.(2.23) in the PR-AB paper apart from the multiplication by the
y

dependent term
H_{0,0}(a_{y},Q_{y})
. We have

\displaystyle H_{0,0}(a,Q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{[a^{2}+Q^{2}]^{1/2}},\;\;\;\;H_{1,1}(a,Q)=\frac{Q}{[a^{2}+Q^{2}]^{3/2}}

Hence

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{K}\theta_{x}Q_{x}{\rm Im}\left\{e^{i(\Phi_{x}-q_{x}/2)}\frac{1}{[a_{x}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}]^{3/2}}\frac{1}{[a_{y}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2}]^{1/2}}\right\}

(15.44)

Writing

\displaystyle[a_{x}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}]^{3/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{x})^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}]^{3/2}\equiv A_{1,x}\exp[-\frac{3}{2}i\Theta_{x}]

(15.45)

\displaystyle A_{1,x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{x}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{x}^{2}]^{3/4},\;\;\;\Theta_{x}(t)={\rm Arctan}[\frac{2\xi_{x}}{1-\xi_{x}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}}]

(15.46)

\displaystyle(a_{y}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{y})^{2}+Q_{y}^{2}]^{1/2}\equiv A_{1,y}\exp[-\frac{1}{2}i\Theta_{y}]

(15.47)

\displaystyle A_{1,y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{y}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{y}^{2}]^{1/4},\;\;\;\Theta_{y}(t)={\rm Arctan}[\frac{2\xi_{y}}{1-\xi_{y}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2}}]

(15.48)

Hence

\displaystyle\langle x(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{K}\theta_{x}Q_{x}\frac{1}{A_{1,x}A_{1,y}}{\rm Im}\left\{e^{i(\Phi_{x}-q_{x}/2)}\exp[i(\frac{3}{2}\Theta_{x}+\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{y})]\right\}

(15.49)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{K}\theta_{x}\frac{Q_{x}}{A_{1,x}A_{1,y}}\sin(\Phi_{x}(t)+\frac{3}{2}\Theta_{x}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{y}(t)-\frac{1}{2}q_{x})

The time dependent amplitude of the echo pulse is

\langle x(t)_{Amp}\rangle=\beta_{K}\theta_{x}\frac{Q_{x}}{A_{1,x}(t)A_{1,y}(t)}=\beta_{K}\theta_{x}\frac{Q_{x}}{[(1-\xi_{x}(t)^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{x}(t)^{2}]^{3/4}[(1-\xi_{y}(t)^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{y}(t)^{2}]^{1/4}}

(15.50)

The amplitude has a maximum when
\xi_{x}(t)=0=\xi_{y}(t)
or at
t=2\tau
, and we have for the echo amplitude

\langle x(2\tau)\rangle^{echoamp}=\beta_{K}\theta_{x}\frac{Q_{x}}{[1+Q_{x}^{2}]^{3/2}[1+Q_{y}^{2}]^{1/2}}

(15.51)

This only modifies the 1D expression by the multiplicative factor
[1+Q_{y}^{2}]^{-1/2}
.
Note that
Q_{x},Q_{y}
are not truly independent variables, since they both refer to the same quadrupole.
Unfortunately, this expression does not depend on the tunes
\nu_{x},\nu_{y}
.

15.2.3
FWHM of the echo pulse

At a time
t=2\tau+\Delta t_{H}
, the echo drops to half its maximum amplitude. At this time, we have

\xi_{x}(t)=w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}\Delta t_{H},\;\;\;\xi_{y}(t)=w_{xy}\epsilon_{y}\Delta t_{H}

In the first approximation, I will assume the linear quadrupole theory for the echo amplitude, which is valid as long as the
quad strength
Q_{x}\leq Q_{x,opt}
where
Q_{x,opt}
is the strength that maximizes the echo. Initially I will also assume that
the echo falls to half the maximum when the amplitude falls to half the maximum amplitude.
In the linear approximation, we have
\langle x(2\tau)\rangle^{echoamp}=\beta_{K}\theta_{x}Q_{x}
and the amplitude falls to half maximum
at

\displaystyle\frac{1}{[(1-\xi_{x}(t)^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{x}(t)^{2}]^{3/4}[(1-\xi_{y}(t)^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{y}(t)^{2}]^{1/4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\frac{1}{[(1+\xi_{x}(t)^{2}]^{3/2}[(1+\xi_{y}(t)^{2}]^{1/2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow[1+\xi_{x}(t)^{2}][1+\xi_{y}^{2}]^{1/3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2/3}

This equation for
\Delta t_{H}
depends only on the emittances
\epsilon_{x},\epsilon_{y}
and the detuning parameter
w_{xx},w_{xy}
and is independent of the other parameters such as the delay and the quad strength.

If we have
\xi_{y}(t)\ll 1
(either because
\epsilon_{y}/\epsilon_{x}\ll 1
, true for electron beams or because
w_{xy}\ll w_{xx}
), we have
the approximation

\displaystyle(1+\xi_{x}(t=2\tau+\Delta t_{H})^{2})[1+\frac{1}{3}\xi_{y}(t=2\tau+\Delta t_{H})^{2}+...]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2/3}

\displaystyle\sqrt{(w_{xx}\epsilon_{x})^{2}+\frac{1}{3}(w_{xy}\epsilon_{y})^{2}}\Delta t_{H}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sqrt{2^{2/3}-1}

where we also dropped the term
(w_{xx}\epsilon_{x})^{2}(w_{xy}\epsilon_{y})^{2}\Delta t_{H}^{4}
.
The FWHM in this (effectively 1D case) is

\Delta t_{FWHM}=2\Delta t_{H}\approx 2\sqrt{\frac{(2^{2/3}-1)}{(w_{xx}\epsilon_{x})^{2}+(1/3)(w_{xy}\epsilon_{y})^{2}}}

(15.52)

This approximation could be slightly improved by keeping the term dropped
(w_{xx}\epsilon_{x})^{2}(w_{xy}\epsilon_{y})^{2}\Delta t_{H}^{4}

and solving a quadratic equation for
\Delta t_{H}^{2}
, This would be apple polishing at best and would not introduce dependences on
other parameters.

A more qualitative improvement would be to include the phase dependent part.
In the linear quadrupole theory, we can simplify the phases as

\displaystyle\Theta_{x}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Arctan}[\frac{2\xi_{x}}{1-\xi_{x}^{2}}]=2{\rm Arctan}[\xi_{x}(t)]\approx 2\xi_{x}(t)\;\;{\rm if}\;\xi_{x}\ll 1

\displaystyle\Theta_{y}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Arctan}[\frac{2\xi_{y}}{1-\xi_{y}^{2}}]=2{\rm Arctan}[\xi_{y}(t)]\approx 2\xi_{y}(t)\;\;{\rm if}\;\xi_{y}\ll 1

Let
t_{H}=2\tau+\Delta t_{H}
. Thus at the half max time, we have instead

\displaystyle\frac{1}{[(1+\xi_{x}(t_{H})^{2}]^{3/2}[(1+\xi_{y}(t_{H})^{2}]^{1/2}}\sin[\omega_{x,0}\Delta t_{H}+\frac{3}{2}\Theta_{x}(t_{H})+\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{y}(t_{H})-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}

(15.53)

where
\xi_{x}(t_{H})=w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}\Delta t_{H},\xi_{y}(t_{H})=w_{xy}\epsilon_{y}\Delta t_{H}
. While this equation most likely has to be solved
numerically, this equation also shows that under the approximations made above, the FWHM does not depend on the quadrupole
strength or the delay time but on the detunings
w_{xx},w_{xy}
and the emittances
\epsilon_{x},\epsilon_{y}
.

15.2.4
The
\langle y\rangle
moment

Let me now calculate
\langle y\rangle
.

\langle y(t)\rangle=\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\sqrt{2\beta_{y}J_{y}}\cos\phi_{y}\psi_{5}(J_{x},\phi_{x},J_{y},\phi_{y},t)

(15.54)

Substituting for
\psi_{5}
from Eq.
, using the same notation and keeping terms to
O(q_{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\langle y(t)\rangle

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{y}}{\beta_{x}}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\sqrt{J_{x}J_{y}}\cos\phi_{y}

\displaystyle\times[1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]\exp[-\frac{J_{x}[1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}[1+q_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\sin(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

Combining trigonometric terms,

\displaystyle\cos\phi_{y}\sin(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

\displaystyle\times[1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\sin[\phi_{y}+\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sin[\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}]\right)

\displaystyle\times(1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\sin\phi_{1y}+\sin\phi_{2y})-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\left[\cos(\phi_{1y}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})-\cos(\phi_{1y}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\cos(\phi_{2y}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})-\cos(\phi_{2y}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})\right]

where

\displaystyle\phi_{1y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{2y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{3y}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{1y}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=\phi_{y}-\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{4y}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{1y}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=3\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{5y}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{2y}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=-\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}+\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

\displaystyle\phi_{6y}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{2y}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=3\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

As in the case for
\langle x\rangle
, we now have

\displaystyle\langle y(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{y}}{\beta_{x}}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\sqrt{J_{x}J_{y}}\cos\phi_{y}

(15.55)

\displaystyle\times\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\left\{\frac{1}{2}(\sin\phi_{1y}+\sin\phi_{2y})-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\left[\cos\phi_{3y}-\cos\phi_{4y}+\cos\phi_{5y}-\cos\phi_{6y}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{y}}{\beta_{x}}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;\sqrt{J_{x}J_{y}}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\left\{\Phi_{1y}+\Phi_{2y}-\Phi_{3y}+\Phi_{4y}-\Phi_{5y}+\Phi_{6y}\right\}

where

\displaystyle\Phi_{1y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{1y}

(15.56)

\displaystyle\Phi_{2y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{2y}

(15.57)

\displaystyle\Phi_{3y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{3y}

(15.58)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{4y}

(15.59)

\displaystyle\Phi_{5y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{5y}

(15.60)

\displaystyle\Phi_{6y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}q_{x}\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{6y}

(15.61)

Thus the integrands in the terms
\Phi_{jy}
, j=1,… 6 are respectively,

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{1y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{1y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i\{\phi_{1y}-i\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-i\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp\left[i\{\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{2y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\sin\phi_{2y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i\{\phi_{2y}-i\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-i\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left(\exp\left[i\{\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{3y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{3y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{\phi_{y}-\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{4y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{4y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{3\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{5y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{5y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{-\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm Int}(\Phi_{6y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]\cos\phi_{6y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp\left[i\{3\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

No new additional complex parameters need to be introduced.

Again using

\int d\phi\exp[im\phi]\exp[ia\sin(2\phi-\theta)]=2\pi J_{-m/2}(a)\exp[i(m/2)\theta]

The difference with the integrations over
\phi_{x},\phi_{y}
for the
\langle y\rangle
moment compared to those for the
\langle x\rangle

moment are that the integers
m
are odd. Thus

\displaystyle\Phi_{1y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Im}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

(15.62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Im}\left(\int d\phi_{x}\exp\left[i\{\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}\}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\times\int d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{\phi_{y}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left(\exp[-i(\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{00})]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy})\exp[i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{y}-c_{00})]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

The phase factor is

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta\phi_{y}-c_{00}=[\omega_{y0}+w_{xy}J_{x}+w_{yy}J_{y}](t-\tau)-[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{y0}(t-\tau)-\omega_{x0}\tau-w_{xx}J_{x}\tau+w_{xy}[J_{x}(t-\tau)-J_{y}\tau]+w_{yy}J_{y}(t-\tau)-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

This phase factor does not in general become small at any particular time
t
, thus there is no echo in the
y
plane.
At
t=2\tau
, this phase factor is

\varphi_{1}(2\tau)=\left(\omega_{y0}-\omega_{x0}-w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}[J_{x}-J_{y}]+w_{yy}J_{y}\right)\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

(15.63)

If the detuning terms
w_{xx}J_{x},....
are small compared to the nominal tunes, then we can approximate

\varphi_{1}(2\tau)\approx\left(\omega_{y0}-\omega_{x0}\right)\tau

which behaves as

\varphi_{1}(2\tau)\rightarrow 0,\;\;\;{\rm when}\;\;\;\omega_{y0}\rightarrow\omega_{x0}

i.e. as the betatron tunes approach each other.

It appears that none of the factors
\Phi_{jy}
will have a vanishing phase, which would require that all have to be evaluated.

\displaystyle\Phi_{2y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Im}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00}\}\right]\exp\left[i\{z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}\}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left(\exp[-i(\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{00})]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\exp[-i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i(-\Delta\phi_{y}-c_{00})]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

The phase factor is

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Delta\phi_{y}-c_{00}=-[\omega_{y0}+w_{xy}J_{x}+w_{yy}J_{y}](t-\tau)-[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}J_{y}]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega_{y0}(t-\tau)+\omega_{x0}\tau+w_{xx}J_{x}\tau+w_{xy}[J_{x}(t-\tau)+J_{y}\tau]+w_{yy}J_{y}(t-\tau)-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

At the time of the x echo,

\varphi_{2}(2\tau)=-\left(\omega_{y0}+\omega_{x0}-w_{xx}J_{x}+w_{xy}[J_{x}+J_{y}]+w_{yy}J_{y}\right)\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

I do not see conditions under which
\varphi_{2}\rightarrow 0
, so this term
\Phi_{2y}
could be dropped.

\displaystyle\Phi_{3y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{\phi_{y}-\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

(15.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[-i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\exp[i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{y}-c_{00})]J_{1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{3\phi_{x}+\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

(15.66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-3\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{3/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[3i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\exp[i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{1}(t)]J_{3/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\displaystyle\Phi_{5y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{-\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}+\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

(15.67)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[-i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\exp[-i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{2}(t)]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

The phase factor is the same as in
\Phi_{2y}
, hence
\Phi_{5y}
could also be dropped.

\displaystyle\Phi_{6y}(J_{x},J_{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Re}\left(\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp\left[i\{3\phi_{x}-\phi_{y}-3\Delta\phi_{x}+z_{xx}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-z_{xy}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}\}\right]\right)

(15.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(-3\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]J_{3/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})\exp[3i\Delta\phi_{x}]J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\exp[-i\Delta\phi_{y}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\pi^{2}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{2}(t)]J_{3/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)

\Phi_{6y}
can also be dropped.

Dropping
\Phi_{2y},

and substituting into Eq.(
15.55
), we have

\displaystyle\langle y(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{y}}{\beta_{x}}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;\sqrt{J_{x}J_{y}}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\left\{\Phi_{1y}-\Phi_{3y}+\Phi_{4y}\right\}

(15.69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{y}}{\beta_{x}}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;\sqrt{J_{x}J_{y}}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\left\{{\rm Im}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{1}(t)]J_{-1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)-{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{1}(t)]J_{1/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{-1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\varphi_{1}(t)]J_{3/2}(z_{xx}J_{x})J_{1/2}(-z_{xy}J_{y})\right)\right\}

At this point it is not clear to me that the contributions from
\Phi_{3y},\Phi_{4y}
can be dropped, so I need to evaluate all three
terms. First, write

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{y0}(t-\tau)-\omega_{x0}\tau-w_{xx}J_{x}\tau+w_{xy}[J_{x}(t-\tau)-J_{y}\tau]+w_{yy}J_{y}(t-\tau)-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}

(15.70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{y0}(t-\tau)-\omega_{x0}\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}-[w_{xx}\tau-w_{xy}(t-\tau)]J_{x}-[w_{xy}\tau-w_{yy}(t-\tau)]J_{y}

Introduce the scaled variables

\displaystyle u_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{x}/\epsilon_{x},\;\;\;u_{y}=J_{y}/\epsilon_{y},\;\;\;\xi_{xy,1}(t)=[w_{xx}\tau-w_{xy}(t-\tau)]\epsilon_{x}

\displaystyle\xi_{xy,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[w_{xy}\tau-w_{yy}(t-\tau)]\epsilon_{y},\;\;\;\Phi_{xy}(t)=\omega_{y0}(t-\tau)-\omega_{x0}\tau

\displaystyle Q_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z_{xx}\epsilon_{x}\simeq q_{x}w_{xx}\epsilon_{x}\tau,\;\;\;Q_{y}=z_{xy}\epsilon_{y}\simeq q_{y}w_{xy}\epsilon_{y}\tau

\displaystyle\varphi_{1}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Phi_{xy}-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}-\xi_{xy,1}u_{x}-\xi_{xy,2}u_{y}

(15.71)

The three integrals are of the form

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left[\left(\int du_{x}\sqrt{u_{x}}\exp[-u_{x}-i\xi_{xy,1}u_{x}]J_{-1/2}(Q_{x}u_{x})\right)\left(\int du_{y}\sqrt{u_{y}}\exp[-u_{y}-i\xi_{xy,2}u_{y}]J_{-1/2}(-Q_{y}u_{y})\right)\right]

(15.72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left[\left(\int du_{x}\sqrt{u_{x}}\exp[-a_{xy,1}u_{x}]J_{-1/2}(Q_{x}u_{x})\right)\left(\int du_{y}\sqrt{u_{y}}\exp[-a_{xy,2}u_{y}]J_{-1/2}(-Q_{y}u_{y})\right)\right]

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left[\left(\int du_{x}\sqrt{u_{x}}\exp[-a_{xy,1}u_{x}]J_{1/2}(Q_{x}u_{x})\right)\left(\int du_{y}\sqrt{u_{y}}\exp[-a_{xy,2}u_{y}]J_{-1/2}(-Q_{y}u_{y})\right)\right]

(15.73)

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left[\left(\int du_{x}\sqrt{u_{x}}\exp[-a_{xy,1}u_{x}]J_{3/2}(Q_{x}u_{x})\right)\left(\int du_{y}\sqrt{u_{y}}\exp[-a_{xy,2}u_{y}]J_{-1/2}(-Q_{y}u_{y})\right)\right]

(15.74)

\displaystyle a_{xy,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+i\xi_{xy,1},\;\;\;\;a_{xy,2}=1+i\xi_{xy,2}

The
u_{y}
integrations are the same in all cases.

Mathematica yields the following

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}du\;\sqrt{u}\exp[-au]J_{-1/2}(Qu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{a}{\sqrt{Q}(a^{2}+Q^{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}du\;\sqrt{u}\exp[-au]J_{-1/2}(-Qu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-i\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{a}{\sqrt{Q}(a^{2}+Q^{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}du\;\sqrt{u}\exp[-au]J_{1/2}(Qu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{\sqrt{Q}}{(a^{2}+Q^{2})}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}du\;\sqrt{u}\exp[-au]J_{3/2}(Qu)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\frac{(a^{2}+Q^{2}){\rm ArcTan}[Q/a]-aQ}{Q^{3/2}(a^{2}+Q^{2})}

Hence

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\pi}{\rm Im}\left[\frac{a_{xy,1}}{\sqrt{Q_{x}}(a_{xy,1}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})}\frac{ia_{xy,2}}{\sqrt{Q_{y}}(a_{xy,2}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{1}{\sqrt{Q_{x}Q_{y}}}{\rm Im}\left[\frac{a_{xy,1}}{(a_{xy,1}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})}\frac{ia_{xy,2}}{(a_{xy,2}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})}\right]

Writing

\displaystyle a_{xy,1}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+i\xi_{xy,1})^{2}+Q_{x}^{2}\equiv A_{xy,1}\exp[i2\Theta_{xy,1}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow A_{xy,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{xy,1}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{xy,1}^{2}]^{1/2},\;\;\;\Theta_{xy,1}={\rm Arctan}[\frac{\xi_{xy,1}}{(1-\xi_{xy,1}^{2}+Q_{x}^{2})}]

\displaystyle a_{xy,2}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+i\xi_{xy,2})^{2}+Q_{y}^{2}\equiv A_{xy,2}\exp[i2\Theta_{xy,2}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow A_{xy,1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[(1-\xi_{xy,2}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})^{2}+4\xi_{xy,2}^{2}]^{1/2},\;\;\;\Theta_{xy,2}={\rm Arctan}[\frac{\xi_{xy,2}}{(1-\xi_{xy,2}^{2}+Q_{y}^{2})}]

15.2.5
Second order moment
\langle x^{2}\rangle

Since
x^{2}=2\beta_{x}J_{x}\cos^{2}\phi_{x}
, we have

\langle x^{2}(t)\rangle=\frac{1}{2}(2\beta_{x})\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}J_{x}[1+\cos 2\phi_{x}]\psi_{5}(J_{x},\phi_{x},J_{y},\phi_{y},t)

(15.75)

With the notations of the previous subsections, we can write the DF from Eq.(
) as

\displaystyle\psi_{5}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{2J_{x}}{\beta_{x}}}[1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]\exp[-\frac{J_{x}[1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}[1+q_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]}{\epsilon_{y}}](1-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})

\displaystyle\times\sin\left(\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-\left[\omega_{x0}+w_{xx}J_{x}(1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})+w_{xy}(1+q_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}})J_{y}\right]\tau-\frac{1}{2}q_{x}\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\sqrt{\frac{2J_{x}}{\beta_{x}}}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}[1-q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}[1+q_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}]}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times[1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]\sin(\phi_{0})

\displaystyle\phi_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00}

Combining the trigonometric terms,

\displaystyle[1-\frac{3}{2}q_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}]\sin(\phi_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}[\cos(\phi_{0}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})-\cos(\phi_{0}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}[\cos(\phi_{0-})-\cos(\phi_{0+})]

\displaystyle\phi_{0-}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{0}-2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=-\phi_{x}+\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

\displaystyle\phi_{0+}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\phi_{0}+2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}=3\phi_{x}-\Delta\phi_{x}+c_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}-c_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}-c_{00})

The second dipole moment in 2D is now

\displaystyle\langle x^{2}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2\beta_{x}}\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;\;\;J_{x}^{3/2}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

(15.76)

\displaystyle\times d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\left\{(1+\cos 2\phi_{x})\left[\sin\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}[\cos(\phi_{0-})-\cos(\phi_{0+})]\right]\right\}

Consider the two contributions separately,

\displaystyle\langle x^{2}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\sqrt{2\beta_{x}}\beta_{K}\theta_{x}}{(2\pi)^{2}\epsilon_{x}^{2}\epsilon_{y}}\int dJ_{x}dJ_{y}\;\;\;J_{x}^{3/2}\exp[-\frac{J_{x}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{J_{y}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\left(I+II\right)

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\left[\sin\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}[\cos(\phi_{0-})-\cos(\phi_{0+})]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]

\displaystyle\times\cos 2\phi_{x}\left[\sin\phi_{0}-\frac{3}{4}q_{x}[\cos(\phi_{0-})-\cos(\phi_{0+})]\right]

Here

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}]\sin\phi_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left\{\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[i\left(\phi_{0}-i\frac{q_{x}J_{x}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}+i\frac{q_{y}J_{y}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Im}\left\{\exp[-i(\Delta\phi_{x}-c_{00})]\int\int d\phi_{x}d\phi_{y}\exp[i\left(\phi_{x}+\frac{z_{xx}\sin 2\phi_{x,-\Delta\phi_{x}}}{\epsilon_{x}}-\frac{z_{xy}\sin 2\phi_{y,-\Delta\phi_{y}}}{\epsilon_{y}}\right)\right\}

16
Spectral Analysis

•

Spectrum with a single kick, and linear analysis

•

Spectrum with a single kick, nonlinear analysis

•

Extracting information about detuning from echo spectrum

•

How else is the echo spectrum useful?

For example: in the presence of energy spread and chromaticity, how would the echo spectrum be affected?
How can energy spread affect echo amplitudes besides the additional tune spread from chromaticity?

Can the echo spectrum reveal something about impedances? How are impedances measured from coherent tune shifts? This likely requires the analysis of echoes in the presence of a resistive wall wake. The impedance will affect the decoherence, shortening the decoherence time. How will it affect the echo amplitude?

•

With the nonlinear theory, does the echo spectrum get affected by the initial amplitude? If so, could the echo spectrum be affected by the presence of, and therefore detect, nearby resonances.

In the absence of diffusion, the time variation of the echo amplitude is
determined by the factor

\displaystyle A_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\xi(3-\xi^{2})\cos\Phi+(1-3\xi^{2})\sin\Phi}{(1+\xi^{2})^{3}}

(16.1)

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{\beta}(t-2\tau),\;\;\;\xi=\omega_{rev}\mu(t-2\tau)

(16.2)

So, the spectrum is determined by the two parameters
\omega_{\beta},\mu
.
The complete echo amplitude is given by
\beta_{K}\theta q\omega^{\prime}\tau\epsilon A_{F}
.

The above amplitude factor can be rewritten as

\displaystyle A_{F}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1+\xi^{2})^{3/2}}\sin(\Phi+\chi)

(16.3)

\displaystyle\tan\chi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\xi(3-\xi^{2})}{(1-3\xi^{2})}

Taking the Fourier transform,

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{F}(\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\;e^{i\omega t}A_{F}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2i}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\;e^{i\omega t}\frac{1}{(1+\xi^{2})^{3/2}}\left[e^{i(\Phi+\chi)}-e^{-i(\Phi+\chi)}\right]

The first term contributes to the negative frequency spectrum while the second contributes
to the positive frequency part. Considering only the second term

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{F}(\omega>0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2i}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\;e^{i\omega t}\frac{1}{(1+\xi^{2})^{3/2}}e^{-i(\Phi+\chi)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2i}e^{i2\omega_{\beta}\tau}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\;e^{i(\omega-\omega_{\beta})t}\frac{1}{(1+\xi^{2})^{3/2}}e^{-i\chi}

This can be evaluated by a contour integration method, see Appendix A.
The result is

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{F}(\omega)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi}{6}\frac{e^{i2(\omega-\omega_{\beta})\tau}}{\mu\omega_{rev}}\delta^{3}e^{-\delta},\;\;\;\;\delta=\frac{\omega-\omega_{\beta}}{\mu\omega_{rev}}\geq 0

(16.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,\;\;\;\;\;\delta<0

(16.5)

From this it follows that the spectrum has a peak at
\delta=3
or at a tune
given by

\nu_{peak}=\nu_{\beta}+3\mu

(16.6)

Thus the peak of the echo spectrum is shifted from the nominal tune
\nu_{\beta}
by three
times the
detuning parameter to one side and there are no frequencies below (if
\mu>0
) the tune
or above (if
\mu<0
) the nominal tune.

The full width at half maximum of the echo spectrum is

\delta_{FWHM}=4.12,\;\;\;\Delta\nu_{Echo,FWHM}=4.12\mu

(16.7)

Figure 1:
Spectrum of the theoretical echo pulse without diffusion

Fig.
1
shows a numerical calculation of the echo spectrum,
This spectrum is obtained with parameters
\nu_{\beta}\equiv\omega_{\beta}/\omega_{rev}=0.245

and the detuning parameter is
\mu=0.0077
. The theoretical peak is expected at

\nu_{\beta}+3\mu=0.268
while the numerical calculation shows the peak at 0.260.

16.1
FFT from simulation

Do an FFT of the echo pulse. How does the spectrum change (in particular the dominant frequency)
as the quad kick is increased upto saturation and beyond?

Li on Jan 2, 2017

I have double-checked my simulation and results, and it seems that they do match your theory
predictions. The broad shape of the peaks was due to the small resolution of the horizontal axis.
Viewed using a range of 0 to 0.5, the peaks appear much more defined. Furthermore, using
mu -0.0012, the location of the peaks matched the predicted 0.241 (bare tune = 0.245). However,
there appears to be no changes to the FFT peak in the saturation regime (see attachment).

17
Longitudinal Echoes

Bunched beam longitudinal echoes have been observed at the AGS (1998) and at HERA (2002).

Action Items: Theory of Bunched Beam Echoes

•

Completely linear theory in both phase and voltage kicks

•

Linear in phase, nonlinear in voltage

•

Nonlinear in phase and voltage

17.1
Longitudinal action angle variables for small amplitudes

The longitudinal action, angle
(J_{s},\varphi)
variables for small amplitude motion as in SY Lee’s book,
pages 234-235 , Eq.(3.73)

\phi-\phi_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{2h\eta J_{s}}{Q_{s}}}\cos\varphi,\;\;\;\delta=-\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}J_{s}}{h\eta}}\sin\varphi

(17.1)

where the zero amplitude synchrotron tune
Q_{s}
is

Q_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{heV_{0}|\eta\cos\phi_{s}|}{2\pi\beta^{2}E}}\equiv\nu_{s}\sqrt{|\cos\phi_{s}|},\;\;\;\;\nu_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{heV_{0}|\eta|}{2\pi\beta^{2}E}}

(17.2)

Introduce the variables

a=\frac{1}{2}\frac{h\eta}{Q_{s}},\;\;\;b=\frac{1}{2}\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}=\frac{1}{4a},\;\;\;\theta=\phi-\phi_{s},

(17.3)

The action and angle variables are

\displaystyle J_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{h\eta}{Q_{s}}[\delta^{2}+(\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta})^{2}(\phi-\phi_{s})^{2}],\;\;\;\;\tan\varphi=-\frac{h\eta}{Q_{s}}\frac{\delta}{\phi-\phi_{s}}

(17.4)

\displaystyle J_{s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2},\;\;\;\varphi=-{\rm Arctan}[2a\frac{\delta}{\theta}]

(17.5)

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{aJ_{s}}\cos\varphi,\;\;\;\;\delta=-2\sqrt{bJ_{s}}\cos\varphi

(17.6)

The averaged Hamiltonian Eq.(3.75) and equations of motion are

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{rev}Q_{s}J_{s}-\frac{\omega_{rev}h\eta}{16}(1+\frac{5}{3}\tan^{2}\phi_{s})J_{s}^{2}+\ldots

(17.7)

\displaystyle\frac{d\varphi}{dt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{rev}\left[Q_{s}-\frac{h\eta}{8}(1+\frac{5}{3}\tan^{2}\phi_{s})J_{s}\right]

(17.8)

\displaystyle\frac{dJ_{s}}{dt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(17.9)

The action dependent tune and frequency are

Q_{s}(J_{s})=Q_{s}-\frac{h\eta}{8}(1+\frac{5}{3}\tan^{2}\phi_{s})J_{s},\;\;\;\;\omega(J_{s})=\omega_{rev}Q_{s}(J_{s}

(17.10)

17.2
Linear theory in both phase and voltage kicks

Using the phase space variables
(\phi,\delta)
, the equations of motion (SY Lee’s book, Eqs (3.35)-(3.36),, pg 224), the
equations of motion and Hamiltonian are

\displaystyle\frac{d\phi}{dt}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h\omega_{rev}\eta\delta,\;\;\;\frac{d\delta}{dt}=\frac{\omega_{rev}}{2\pi}\frac{eV_{0}}{\beta^{2}E}(\sin\phi-\sin\phi_{s})

(17.11)

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h\omega_{rev}\eta\delta^{2}+\frac{\omega_{rev}}{2\pi}\frac{eV_{0}}{\beta^{2}E}[\cos\phi-\cos\phi_{s}+(\phi-\phi_{s})\sin\phi_{s}]

(17.12)

h
is the harmonic number
We assume that the initial distribution is determined entirely by the longitudinal action
J_{s}(\phi,\delta)
, so that

\psi(\phi,\delta)=\psi_{0}(J_{s})

(17.13)

At time
t=0
, kick the phase by
\Delta\phi_{k}
so that after the kick, the variables are

\phi(t=0+)=\phi+\Delta\phi_{k},\;\;\;\;\delta(t=0+)=\delta

and the distribution function is

\psi_{1}(\phi,\delta)=\psi_{0}(J_{s}(\phi-\Delta\phi_{k},\delta)

(17.14)

Linearizing in the kick,

\psi_{1}(\phi,\delta)=\psi_{0}(J_{s})-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\Delta\phi_{k}

The variables after the kick evolve as
J_{s}={\rm const},\phi(t)=\phi(0)+h\omega_{rev}\eta\int\delta(t)\;dt
.
These equations are not convenient to use.

In action angle variables, we have

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(J_{s},\varphi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(J_{s}(\phi-\Delta\phi_{k})),

(17.15)

\displaystyle J_{s}(\phi-\Delta\phi_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{h\eta}{Q_{s}}[\delta^{2}+(\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta})^{2}(\phi-\Delta\phi_{k}-\phi_{s})^{2}]

(17.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{s}-\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}(\phi-\phi_{s})\Delta\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}(\Delta\phi_{k})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{s}-\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}J_{s}}{h\eta}}\cos\varphi\Delta\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}(\Delta\phi_{k})^{2}

After the kick, the action angle variables evolve as
J_{s}(t)=J_{s}
,
\varphi(t)=\varphi+\omega(J_{s})t
, hence the
distribution function is

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(J_{s},\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(J_{s},\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)

(17.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(J_{s}-\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}J_{s}}{h\eta}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)\Delta\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}(\Delta\phi_{k})^{2})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(J_{s})-\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})[\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}J_{s}}{h\eta}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)\Delta\phi_{k}]

(17.18)

In the last expression, I have dropped the term in
(\Delta\phi_{k})^{2}
.

The beam current monitor measures the zeroth moment as

\displaystyle I_{2}(\phi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\psi_{2}(\delta,\phi)\;d\delta

(17.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{0}(\phi)-\Delta\phi_{k}\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}}{h\eta}}\int\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})[\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)]d\delta

The time dependent part of the current is given by the second term above, so the 1st term is dropped.

Now I will assume that

\psi_{0}(J_{s})=\frac{1}{2\pi J_{s,0}}\exp[-J_{s}/J_{s,0}]

(17.20)

Note: This results in a Gaussian in both
\phi
and
\delta
. This should be OK in
\delta
but not so much in
\phi
.

The change in current is

I_{2}(\phi,t)=\frac{\Delta\phi_{k}}{2\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{2Q_{s}}{h\eta}}\int\exp[-J_{s}/J_{s,0}]\left[\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)\right]d\delta

(17.21)

Write

\omega(J_{s})=\omega_{s}+\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s},\;\;\;\omega_{s}^{\prime}=-\frac{h\omega_{rev}\eta}{8}(1+\frac{5}{3}\tan^{2}\phi_{s})

Use

\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos\varphi=\sqrt{\frac{Q_{s}}{2h\eta}}\theta=\sqrt{b}\theta,\;\;\;\sqrt{J_{s}}\sin\varphi=-\sqrt{\frac{h\eta}{2Q_{s}}}\delta=-\sqrt{a}\delta

Expand

\displaystyle\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos\varphi\cos\omega(J_{s})t+\sqrt{J_{s}}\sin\varphi\sin\omega(J_{s})t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{b}\theta\cos[\omega_{s}t+\omega_{s}^{\prime}(a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2})t]-\sqrt{a}\delta\sin[\omega_{s}t+\omega_{s}^{\prime}(a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2})t]

Hence

\displaystyle I_{2}(\phi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta\phi_{k}}{2\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}\sqrt{4b}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp[-J_{s}/J_{s,0}]

(17.22)

\displaystyle\times\left[\sqrt{b}\theta\cos[\omega_{s}t+\omega_{s}^{\prime}(a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2})t]-\sqrt{a}\delta\sin[\omega_{s}t+\omega_{s}^{\prime}(a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2})t]\right]\;d\delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta\phi_{k}}{2\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}2b\theta\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp[-J_{s}/J_{s,0}]\left(\cos[\omega_{s}t+\omega_{s}^{\prime}(a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2})t]\right)\;d\delta

where in the last step we used the fact that the second integrand is an odd function and the integral vanishes. Using

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp[-px^{2}]\;dx=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{p}}

we have

\displaystyle I_{2}(\phi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b\theta\Delta\phi_{k}}{\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}\exp[-\frac{b\theta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\omega_{s}t+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2}t)]\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\exp[a(-1/J_{s,0}+i\omega_{s}^{\prime}t)\delta^{2}]\;d\delta\right)

(17.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b\theta\Delta\phi_{k}}{\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}\sqrt{\pi}\exp[-\frac{b\theta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]\sqrt{\frac{J_{s,0}}{a}}{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})t]\sqrt{\frac{1}{[1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]}}\right)

We can write

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{[1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{[1+i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]}{1+(\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t)^{2}}}

\displaystyle 1+i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{1}(t)\exp[i\Phi_{1}(t)]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\sqrt{\frac{1}{[1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{\exp[i\Phi_{1}(t)]}{A_{1}(t)}}

where

A_{1}(t)=\sqrt{1+(\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t)^{2}},\;\;\;\;\Phi_{1}(t)={\rm Arctan}[\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]

(17.24)

which leads to

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})t]\sqrt{\frac{1}{[1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}J_{s,0}t]}}\right)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(t)}}\cos[(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})t+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}(t)]

Hence

\displaystyle I_{2}(\phi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b\theta\Delta\phi_{k}}{\sqrt{\pi}\sqrt{a}J_{s,0}^{3/2}}\exp[-\frac{b\theta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(t)}}\cos[(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})t+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}(t)]

(17.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\theta\Delta\phi_{k}}{\sqrt{\pi}}(\frac{b}{J_{s,0}})^{3/2}\exp[-\frac{b\theta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(t)}}\cos[(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})t+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}(t)]

The amplitude of the current modulation is

I_{2}(\phi,t)^{amp}=\frac{2\theta\Delta\phi_{k}}{\sqrt{\pi}}(\frac{b}{J_{s,0}})^{3/2}\exp[-\frac{b\theta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(t)}}

(17.26)

As a function of
\theta=\phi-\phi_{s}
, it starts from zero at
\theta=0
, reaches a maximum at
\theta=\sqrt{J_{s,0}/(2b)}

or at

\phi_{max}-\phi_{s}=\sqrt{\frac{h\eta J_{s,0}}{Q_{s}}}

The modulation effectively vanishes for

\sqrt{b/J_{s,0}}\theta_{large}\geq\pi,\;\;\;\Rightarrow\phi_{large}-\phi_{s}\geq\sqrt{\frac{2h\eta J_{s,0}}{Q_{s}}}\pi

This suggests that if the bunch length
\sigma_{\phi}\leq\phi_{max}-\phi_{s}
, the maximum of the modulation may be outside the
bunch. Preferably, one would want
\sigma_{\phi}\geq\phi_{large}-\phi_{s}
.
These features can be seen in Fig.
2
.

Figure 2:
The function
\theta\exp[-\theta^{2}]
as a function of
\theta
.

CHECK: Can the current
I_{2}
be calculated without the Taylor expansion and linearizing in
\Delta\theta_{k}
? It is possible in the
transverse case. see Chao’s notes.

Applying the voltage kick at time
\tau

At time
\tau
, the distribution function is

\displaystyle\psi_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(t=\tau)=\psi_{0}(J_{s}-2\sqrt{bJ_{s}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)\Delta\phi_{k}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{Q_{s}}{h\eta}(\Delta\phi_{k})^{2})

(17.27)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(J_{s})-2\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\Delta\phi_{k}\sqrt{bJ_{s}}\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

(17.28)

The kick changes the energy variable to

\displaystyle\delta(\tau_{+})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta(\tau)+\frac{eV_{k}}{\beta^{2}E}[\sin(h_{k}\phi)-\sin(h_{k}\phi_{s})]\equiv\delta(\tau)+\Delta\delta_{k}

(17.29)

\displaystyle\Delta\delta_{k}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{eV_{k}}{\beta^{2}E}[\sin(h_{k}\phi)--\sin(h_{k}\phi_{s})]=q_{k}[\sin(h_{k}\phi)-\sin(h_{k}\phi_{s})],\;\;\;q_{k}\equiv\frac{eV_{k}}{\beta^{2}E}

(17.30)

where
(V_{k},h_{k})
are the voltage and harmonic number of the kick. If needed, I could linearize the
\Delta\delta_{k}
parameter
for small amplitudes about
\phi_{s}
. Write
\phi=\phi_{s}+\theta
, where
\theta\ll 1
then

\displaystyle\sin\phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sin\phi_{s}\cos\theta+\sin\theta\cos\phi_{s}\approx\sin\phi_{s}+\cos\phi_{s}\theta

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\Delta\delta_{k}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle(q_{k}h_{k}\cos\phi_{s})\theta\equiv r_{k}\theta,\;\;\;\;r_{k}=q_{k}h_{k}\cos\phi_{s}

The distribution function after the kick is

\displaystyle\psi_{4}(\phi,\delta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{3}(\phi,\delta-\Delta\delta_{k})\approx\psi_{3}(J_{s},\varphi)-\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial\delta}\Delta\delta_{k}

(17.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{3}(J_{s},\varphi)-[\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial dJ_{s}}\frac{\partial J_{s}}{\partial\delta}+\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial d\varphi}\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial\delta}]\Delta\delta_{k}

From the definitions we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial J_{s}}{\partial\delta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2a\delta=-4a\sqrt{bJ_{s}}\sin\varphi=-\sqrt{\frac{J_{s}}{b}}\sin\varphi

\displaystyle\sec^{2}\varphi\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial\delta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2a\frac{1}{\theta},\;\;

\displaystyle;\Rightarrow\frac{\partial\varphi}{\partial\delta}=-2a\frac{1}{2\sqrt{a}}\cos\varphi=-\sqrt{a}\cos\varphi

From Eq.(
17.28
)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial dJ_{s}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})-2\sqrt{b}\Delta\phi_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial dJ_{s}}\left[\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\sqrt{J_{s}}\right]\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{b}\Delta\phi_{k}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau\sqrt{J_{s}}]\sin(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial d\varphi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{b}\Delta\phi_{k}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\omega(J_{s})\tau\sqrt{J_{s}}]\cos(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

For sufficiently long times
\tau
so that

|\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau J_{s,0}|\gg 1

the third term in
\partial\psi_{3}/\partial J_{s}
will dominate all the other terms (the same argument as in Chao). Keeping only this term

\displaystyle\psi_{4}(J_{s},\varphi)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\Delta\delta_{k}\frac{\partial\psi_{3}}{\partial dJ_{s}}\frac{\partial J_{s}}{\partial\delta}\approx-2\sqrt{b}\Delta\phi_{k}\Delta\delta_{k}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau\sqrt{J_{s}}]\sin(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)\sqrt{\frac{J_{s}}{b}}\sin\varphi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Delta\phi_{k}\Delta\delta_{k}\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau J_{s}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\sin(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

Using the linearized form of the kick
\Delta\delta_{k}=r_{k}\theta=2r_{k}\sqrt{aJ_{s}}\cos\varphi
to finally obtain

\psi_{4}(J_{s},\varphi)\approx 2\sqrt{a}\Delta\phi_{k}r_{k}\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau J_{s}^{3/2}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\sin 2\varphi\sin(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})\tau)

(17.32)

This closely resembles Eq.(26) in Chao’s notes for the transverse case.

At time
t>\tau
after the quad like kick, the distribution function is

\displaystyle\psi_{5}(J_{s},\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\psi_{4}(J_{s},\varphi-\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau))

(17.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{a}\Delta\phi_{k}r_{k}\omega^{\prime}(J_{s})\tau J_{s}^{3/2}\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})\sin[2\varphi-2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)]\sin[\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t]

The zeroth moment or beam current
I_{5}
is

\displaystyle I_{5}(\phi,t>\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\psi_{5}(J_{s},\varphi,t)\;d\delta

We have on expanding (as done for
I_{2}
)

\sqrt{J_{s}}\sin(\varphi-\omega(J_{s})t)=-\sqrt{a}\delta\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]-\sqrt{b}\theta\sin[\omega(J_{s})t

Using
\psi_{0}^{\prime}(J_{s})=-\exp[-J_{s}/J_{s,0}]/(2\pi J_{s,0}^{2})

\displaystyle I_{5}(\phi,t>\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}2\sqrt{a}\Delta\phi_{k}r_{k}\omega^{\prime}\tau\int d\delta\;J_{s}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\sin[2\varphi-2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)]

\displaystyle\left\{\sqrt{a}\delta\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]-\sqrt{b}\theta\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]\right\}

Expand

\displaystyle J_{s}\sin[2\varphi-2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{s}[\sin 2\varphi\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)-\cos 2\varphi\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\sqrt{J_{s}}\sin\varphi)(\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos\varphi)\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)-(2(\sqrt{J_{s}}\cos\varphi)^{2}-J_{s})\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sqrt{ab}\theta\delta\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)-(2b\theta^{2}-J_{s})\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)

We have
2\sqrt{ab}=1
. Hence

\displaystyle J_{s}\sin[2\varphi-2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)]\left\{\sqrt{a}\delta\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]-\sqrt{b}\theta\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[-\theta\delta\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)-(2b\theta^{2}-J_{s})\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\right]\left\{\sqrt{a}\delta\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]-\sqrt{b}\theta\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]\right\}

In the integrand for
I_{5}
, we can drop all the odd functions of
\delta
. Note that
\omega(J_{s})
is an even function of
\delta
.
Hence

\displaystyle I_{5}(\phi,t>\tau)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi J_{s,0}^{2}}2\sqrt{a}\Delta\phi_{k}r_{k}\omega^{\prime}\tau\int d\delta\;\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\left\{\sqrt{b}\theta(2b\theta^{2}-J_{s})\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{a}\theta\delta^{2}\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]\right\}

Use

\displaystyle\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\sin\omega(J_{s})t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\cos[\omega(J_{s})(t-2\tau)]-\cos[\omega(J_{s})(3t-2\tau)]\right)

\displaystyle\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\cos\omega(J_{s})t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\cos[\omega(J_{s})(t-2\tau)]+\cos[\omega(J_{s})(3t-2\tau)]\right)

Since
J_{s}=a\delta^{2}+b\theta^{2}

2b\theta^{2}-J_{s}=b\theta^{2}-a\delta^{2}

Let

\tau_{1}=t-2\tau,\;\;\;\tau_{2}=3t-2\tau

then

\displaystyle\cos[\omega(J_{s})(t-2\tau)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos[(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]\equiv\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]

\displaystyle\cos[\omega(J_{s})(3t-2\tau)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos[(\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2})\tau_{2}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\equiv\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]

\displaystyle\omega_{+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega_{s}+b\omega_{s}^{\prime}\theta^{2}

the integration terms are

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b\sqrt{b}\theta^{3}\int d\delta\;\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}b\sqrt{b}\theta^{3}\int d\delta\;\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\left(\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]-\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}b\sqrt{b}\theta^{3}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]\int d\delta\;\exp[-\frac{a\delta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{1}]\exp[ia\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]-\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{2}]\exp[ia\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right)

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-a\sqrt{b}\theta\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\sin 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\sin[\omega(J_{s})t]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}a\sqrt{b}\theta\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\left(\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]-\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right)

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sqrt{a}\theta\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\cos 2\omega(J_{s})(t-\tau)\cos[\omega(J_{s})t]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{a}\theta\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\left(\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]+\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right)

\displaystyle T_{2}+T_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\theta\sqrt{a}\left[(\sqrt{ab}+1)\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(-\sqrt{ab}+1)\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{J_{s}}{J_{s,0}}]\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+a\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\theta\sqrt{a}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]

\displaystyle\times\int d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-\frac{a\delta^{2}}{J_{s,0}}]{\rm Re}\left(3\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{1}]\exp[ia\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{1}]+\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{2}]\exp[ia\omega_{s}^{\prime}\delta^{2}\tau_{2}]\right)

where I used
\sqrt{ab}=1/2
.

There are 2 different integrals

\displaystyle In_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\delta\;\exp[-(A+iB)\delta^{2}]=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{A+iB}}

\displaystyle In_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\delta\;\delta^{2}\exp[-(A+iB)\delta^{2}]=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{4(A+iB)^{3}}}

Hence

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}b\sqrt{\pi b}\theta^{3}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{1}]\sqrt{\frac{J_{s,0}}{a(1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}\tau_{1}]}}-\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{2}]\sqrt{\frac{J_{s,0}}{a(1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}\tau_{2}]}}\right)

while

\displaystyle T_{2}+T_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\theta\sqrt{\pi a}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]

\displaystyle{\rm Re}\left(3\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{1}]\sqrt{(\frac{J_{s,0}}{a(1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}\tau_{1}]})^{3}}+\exp[i\omega_{+}\tau_{2}]\sqrt{(\frac{J_{s,0}}{a(1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}\tau_{2}]})^{3}}\right)

We had earlier obtained

\sqrt{\frac{1}{(1-i\omega_{s}^{\prime}\tau_{1}]}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(\tau_{1})}}\exp[\frac{1}{2}i\Phi_{1}(\tau_{1})]

Hence

\displaystyle T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}b\sqrt{\pi b}\sqrt{\frac{J_{s,0}}{a}}\theta^{3}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(\tau_{1})}}\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}(\tau_{1})]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(\tau_{2})}}\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Phi_{1}(\tau_{2})]\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{8}\theta\sqrt{\pi a}\sqrt{(\frac{J_{s,0}}{a})^{3}}\exp[-b\theta^{2}/J_{s,0}]

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{\sqrt{A_{1}(\tau_{1})^{3}}}\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{1}+\frac{3}{2}\Phi_{1}(\tau_{1})]+\frac{1}{\sqrt{A_{1}(\tau_{2})^{3}}}\cos[\omega_{+}\tau_{2}+\frac{3}{2}\Phi_{1}(\tau_{2})]\right)

18
Conclusions

In this paper, we have provided the theoretical foundation for several aspects of beam echoes. Numerical validation of some of these results are available in the references listed below. Similar validation of the newer results will follow in
forthcoming publications. There are very many open areas that need a full theoretical formulation, these are left to the imagination of the reader.
